Chapter 14 · Topic 3

Darwinism

EvolutionGenetics & EvolutionStep-by-step Topic
Darwin (Origin of Species, 1859) proposed two ideas: branching descent (all life shares common ancestors - one great Tree of Life) and natural selection (overproduction, variation, competition, survival of the fittest, inheritance of useful traits). The Beagle voyage supplied finches, fossils and barnacles; Malthus supplied the struggle logic. What Darwin lacked - the source of variation and inheritance mechanism - Mendel and genetics later provided.

Topic in Depth · Darwinism

Darwin proposed branching descent (one Tree of Life from common ancestors) plus natural selection (overproduction, variation, competition, fittest survival, inheritance of winners) - Beagle finches, fossils and barnacles supplying patterns, Malthus supplying struggle logic. Missing pieces (variation source, inheritance mechanism) waited for Mendel plus genetics; the modern synthesis fused them. Social Darwinism (applying selection to human societies) is ideology, not Darwin science - examiners occasionally test the distinction.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Branching descent plus natural selection - pattern plus process.
  • Variation plus inheritance gaps filled later by genetics.
Remember
  • Survival of the fittest was a Spencer phrase Darwin adopted.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Darwin voyage observations came mainly from the

Solution: Galapagos finches, fossils and coral atolls seeded Origin of Species (1859).

Index / Genetics & Evolution
Chapter 14 · Topic 4

Natural selection

EvolutionGenetics & EvolutionStep-by-step Topic
Natural selection in action: industrial melanism (soot-darkened bark favoured dark moths - selection reversed as air cleaned), DDT/antibiotic resistance (pre-existing mutants selected within months, never created by the chemical), beak-size shifts after droughts (measured in real time). Selection modes: stabilising (middle wins - human birth weight), directional (edge wins - resistance), disruptive (both edges - finch beak split). Artificial selection is the same force with human goals.

Topic in Depth · Natural selection

Natural selection filters pre-existing variation: industrial melanism (soot favoured dark moths, clean air reversed it), DDT/antibiotic resistance (chemicals select resistant mutants within months, never create them), drought beak shifts (measured real-time). Modes reshape trait curves - stabilising (middle wins: human birth weight), directional (edge wins: resistance), disruptive (both edges: finch beak split). Artificial selection is the same force with human goals (dairy, broilers); sexual selection (peacock tails) can oppose survival selection.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Selects existing variants; never creates mutations.
  • Stabilising centres, directional shifts, disruptive splits.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Antibiotic resistance evolves because antibiotics

Solution: Chemicals filter standing variation - resistance predates exposure.

Index / Genetics & Evolution
Chapter 14 · Topic 5

Modern synthetic theory

EvolutionGenetics & EvolutionStep-by-step Topic
Darwin (Voyage of the Beagle; Origin of Species, 1859): branching descent + natural selection — overproduction, variation, competition, survival of the fittest (Herbert Spencer's phrase), inheritance of useful traits. Living proofs: industrial melanism (dark moths camouflaged on soot), DDT-resistant mosquitoes and antibiotic-resistant bacteria (selection within months). Modern synthetic theory fuses Darwin with genetics: evolution = change in gene frequency via mutation (de Vries, raw material), recombination, gene flow/migration, genetic drift (bottleneck + founder effects, strong in small populations) and natural selection.
Common mistakes: Lamarck's giraffe (use and disuse) is rejected — acquired characters are not inherited.

Deeper Understanding · Three modes of selection

Graph beak size vs fitness: stabilising selection peaks in the middle (human birth weight - extremes die), directional shifts the peak aside (DDT resistance, industrial melanism), disruptive splits it in two (finches with small or large beaks but no medium, when medium seeds vanish). Artificial selection is human-driven directional selection (dairy cows, broilers); sexual selection (peacock tails) can even oppose survival selection. Antibiotic resistance evolves by selection on pre-existing mutants, never by antibiotics creating mutations - a subtle but heavily tested distinction.

Topic in Depth · Modern synthetic theory

Modern synthesis fuses Darwin with genetics: evolution equals gene-frequency change driven by mutation (ultimate new alleles), recombination (shuffling), gene flow (migration homogenising), drift (small-population wandering), selection (adaptive filtering) - plus isolation enabling speciation. Fisher-Haldane-Wright mathematised it; Dobzhansky (genetics plus naturalists) and Mayr (biological species concept: interbreeding isolation) completed the architecture. Nothing in biology makes sense except in its light - Dobzhansky dictum.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Gene-frequency change via five forces plus isolation.
  • Mutation creates, recombination shuffles, selection filters, drift wanders.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Biological species concept defines species by

Solution: Mayr: actually or potentially interbreeding populations reproductively isolated from others.
Back to Index
Index / Genetics & Evolution
Chapter 14 · Topic 6

Mutation

EvolutionGenetics & EvolutionStep-by-step Topic
In evolution, mutation is the ultimate raw material: recombination and assortment only reshuffle existing alleles, while mutation alone creates new ones (de Vries called them sports). Most mutations are neutral, many harmful, few beneficial - but populations play a numbers game over millions of generations. Mutation rates (~10^-8 per base per generation) seem tiny until multiplied by billions of individuals and deep time - inevitability, not miracle.

Topic in Depth · Mutation

Evolutionarily, mutation is the only ultimate allele source: recombination reshuffles existing cards, mutation prints new ones (de Vries sports). Rates (~10^-8 per base per generation) seem tiny until multiplied by billions of individuals over millions of generations - inevitability through numbers. Most mutations neutral (molecular clock ticks), many harmful (purged), few beneficial (swept) - and beneficial ones drive every adaptation story from beaks to brains.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Only mutation creates alleles; all else reshuffles.
  • Neutral majority clocks time; beneficial minority drives change.
Remember
  • de Vries rediscovered Mendel plus proposed mutation theory - dual legacy.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Ultimate source of new alleles is

Solution: All other forces rearrange existing variation; mutation alone invents.
Back to Index
Index / Genetics & Evolution
Chapter 14 · Topic 7

Genetic drift

EvolutionGenetics & EvolutionStep-by-step Topic
Genetic drift is random allele-frequency wandering from sampling error - powerful in small populations, negligible in large ones. Bottleneck effect (crash survivors, e.g. cheetahs - near-zero diversity) and founder effect (new colony gene pool, e.g. island species, Amish disorders) both fix alleles by accident, not merit. Drift can even fix harmful alleles - the signature that chance, not selection, ruled.

Topic in Depth · Genetic drift

Genetic drift is sampling-error wandering - powerful in small populations (founder colonies, crash survivors), negligible in large ones. Bottleneck effect (cheetah near-zero diversity after ancient crash) and founder effect (island species, Amish disorders from few settlers) fix alleles by accident, even harmful ones - chance, not merit. Conservation breeding maximises effective population size precisely to outrun drift; captive pedigrees track kinship to avoid it.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Small populations wander; large ones hold steady.
  • Bottleneck (crash) and founder (colony) both slash diversity.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Genetic drift operates most strongly in

Solution: Sampling error swings frequencies wildly when headcounts are low.
Back to Index
Index / Genetics & Evolution
Chapter 14 · Topic 8

Gene flow

EvolutionGenetics & EvolutionStep-by-step Topic
Gene flow (migration) moves alleles between populations - pollen blowing fields over, animals dispersing, humans travelling - homogenising gene pools and opposing speciation. Enough flow merges populations; barriers (rivers, mountains, behaviour, chromosomes) cut flow and let divergence accumulate into new species. Conservation corridors exist precisely to maintain gene flow for endangered fragmented populations.

Topic in Depth · Gene flow

Gene flow (migration) moves alleles between populations - wind-blown pollen fields over, dispersing animals, travelling humans - homogenising gene pools and opposing speciation. Enough flow merges diverging groups; barriers (rivers, mountains, behaviour, chromosomes, timing) cut flow and let differences accumulate toward new species. Hybrid zones (narrow interbreeding borders) visualise flow-versus-selection balance in real landscapes; wildlife corridors exist to preserve flow for fragmented endangered populations.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Migration homogenises; barriers isolate toward speciation.
  • Flow opposes divergence; isolation enables it.
Remember
  • Speciation needs isolation; flow is its enemy.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Gene flow between populations tends to

Solution: Migrant alleles blend populations; only barriers let divergence accumulate.

Index / Genetics & Evolution
Chapter 14 · Topic 9

Hardy-Weinberg principle

EvolutionGenetics & EvolutionStep-by-step Topic
Allele frequencies stay constant (genetic equilibrium) when mating is random and there is no selection, mutation, migration or drift: p² + 2pq + q² = 1 (with p + q = 1). Any disturbance signals evolution in progress. Adaptive radiation: one ancestor diversifies rapidly — Darwin's finches (beaks), Australian marsupials.

In a population, 16% show a recessive trait. Find allele frequencies and carrier percentage.

Answer: q² = 0.16 → q = 0.4, p = 0.6; carriers 2pq = 48%.

Deeper Understanding · Gene pool arithmetic

The gene pool is every allele in a population; evolution is its changing frequency. Five disturbers of Hardy-Weinberg each leave signatures: selection (directional shifts), mutation (new alleles, slow), migration/gene flow (populations converge), drift (small populations wander randomly), non-random mating (inbreeding raises homozygosity without changing allele frequency - the exception students forget). Founder effect (new colony, e.g. island species) and bottleneck (crash survivors, e.g. cheetahs) both slash diversity through drift.

Related Diagram · Hardy-Weinberg jars

Fig: Random mating reshuffles the same alleles every generation - frequencies freeze without disturbance.

Topic in Depth · Hardy-Weinberg principle

Hardy-Weinberg states allele frequencies freeze (p2+2pq+q2=1, p+q=1) under random mating with no selection, mutation, migration or drift - evolution defined as any disturbance. Worked pattern: 16 percent recessive phenotype means q2=0.16, q=0.4, p=0.6, carriers 2pq=48 percent. Non-random mating (inbreeding) raises homozygosity without changing allele frequencies - the exception proving the rule. Deviations diagnose which force operates in real populations.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Equilibrium needs random mating plus zero evolutionary forces.
  • 16 percent recessive means 48 percent carriers - the classic computation.
HW math
PointDetail
GivenRecessive phenotype percent = q2
Step 1q = square root, p = 1-q
Carriers2pq (heterozygotes)
Dominant phenotypep2 + 2pq
NEET Practice MCQ
1

If 9 percent show recessive trait, carrier frequency is

Solution: q2=0.09 gives q=0.3, p=0.7; 2pq=42 percent.

Chapter 14 · Topic 10

Adaptive radiation

EvolutionGenetics & EvolutionStep-by-step Topic
Adaptive radiation is one ancestor diversifying rapidly into empty niches: Darwin finches (beaks for seeds, insects, cactus), Australian marsupials (mole-, mouse-, wolf-like parallels to placentals), Hawaiian honeycreepers. It needs opportunity (islands, post-extinction emptiness) plus variation - and it demonstrates divergent evolution with homologous bones reshaped per lifestyle. Mass extinctions reset the board for the next radiation.

Topic in Depth · Adaptive radiation

Adaptive radiation explodes one ancestor into many niches when opportunity opens: Darwin finches (beak tools for seeds, insects, cactus on Galapagos), Australian marsupials (mole-mouse-wolf parallels to placentals), Hawaiian honeycreepers, cichlid fishes. Requirements: colonisation plus variation plus empty niches (islands, post-extinction voids). It demonstrates divergent evolution with homologous bones reshaped per lifestyle - and mass extinctions reset the board for each new radiation.
NEET Snapshot:
  • One ancestor, many niches, rapid divergence - needs opportunity.
  • Finches (beaks), marsupials (parallel forms) are textbook cases.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Australian marsupials demonstrate

Solution: One marsupial ancestor radiated into mole-, mouse-, wolf-like forms paralleling placentals.

Index / Genetics & Evolution
Chapter 14 · Topic 11

Human evolution

EvolutionGenetics & EvolutionStep-by-step Topic
Dryopithecus/Ramapithecus (hairy, ape-like) → Australopithecus (~2 mya, first human-like, hunted with stone tools) → Homo habilis (650–800 cc brain, first human-like being, no large-scale hunting yet) → Homo erectus (~900 cc, ate meat) → Neanderthal (~1400 cc, Near East/central Asia, protected and buried the dead) → Homo sapiens (arose ~75,000–10,000 years ago, spread worldwide; agriculture ~10,000 years back).
NEET facts:
  • Brain capacity trend: 650 → 900 → 1400 cc (habilis → erectus → Neanderthal).
  • Neanderthals wore animal hides and knew fire and burial.

Deeper Understanding · Neanderthal vs Cro-Magnon

Neanderthals (~1400 cc brains, stocky cold-adapted bodies) buried dead, used fire and wore hides but vanished ~40,000 years ago - modern non-Africans still carry 1-2% Neanderthal DNA from interbreeding. Cro-Magnon (early Homo sapiens in Europe) painted caves (Lascaux), carved tools and founded art - behaviourally modern humans. Agriculture (~10,000 years ago) then exploded populations and diseases together: dense settlements bred epidemics, the start of the human-disease story continued in the health chapter.

Topic in Depth · Human evolution

Human journey: Dryopithecus-Ramapithecus (ape-like hairy ancestors) to Australopithecus (~2 mya, upright, stone tools) to Homo habilis (650-800 cc, first human-like) to Homo erectus (~900 cc, meat, fire) to Neanderthal (~1400 cc, burials, hides) to Homo sapiens (75,000-10,000 years ago, art, language, agriculture 10,000 years back). Brain expansion plus tool complexity plus social cooperation compounded; Neanderthal DNA (1-2 percent in non-Africans) proves interbreeding, not just replacement.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Brain 650 to 900 to 1400 cc; tools, fire, burial, art sequence.
  • Agriculture 10,000 years ago exploded populations plus diseases.
Remember
  • Cro-Magnon painted caves; Neanderthals buried dead - behaviour fossilises too.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

First human-like being with 650-800 cc brain using stone tools was

Solution: Handy Man hunted small game with fashioned tools - humanity first draft.

Chapter 15 · Step-by-step

Human Health and Disease

Every disease, immunity, AIDS, cancer, allergy and drugs.

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareChapter Introduction

Subtopics in this Chapter

1. Health2. Diseases3. Pathogens4. Bacterial diseases5. Viral diseases6. Protozoan diseases7. Helminthic diseases8. Malaria9. Typhoid10. Pneumonia11. Amoebiasis12. Ascariasis13. Filariasis14. Immunity15. Antibodies16. Vaccination17. AIDS18. Cancer19. Allergy20. Autoimmune diseases21. Drug and alcohol abuse

Deep Dive · Malaria life cycle and immunity map

Plasmodium journey (Haemozoin included): an infected female Anopheles injects sporozoites (the infective stage for humans) with saliva. Sporozoites multiply in liver cells, then burst out as merozoites that invade RBCs - digesting haemoglobin and releasing toxic haemozoin, which causes the classic chill-and-fever cycles. Some merozoites become gametocytes; a new mosquito bite carries them to its gut, where fertilisation and sporogony remake sporozoites. P. falciparum causes malignant tertian malaria (most dangerous); benign tertian is P. vivax/ovale, quartan is P. malariae.
Immunity at a glance
TypeSourceMemory?
Active naturalAfter infectionYes - lasting memory cells
Active artificialVaccinationYes - without disease
Passive naturalMother's IgA in colostrumNo - temporary
Passive artificialAntivenom, anti-tetanus serumNo - ready-made antibodies
B-cells give humoral immunity (antibodies: IgG most abundant, IgA in colostrum, IgM first responder, IgE allergy, IgD unknown); T-cells give cell-mediated immunity (helper T cells coordinate, killer T cells destroy grafts and infected cells). Allergy = IgE + histamine/serotonin from mast cells. HIV (retrovirus with reverse transcriptase) destroys helper T (CD4) cells; diagnosis by ELISA; no cure, only anti-retroviral management.
Fig: Two-host cycle - asexual multiplication in humans (liver then RBC), sexual phase in the female Anopheles gut.
Disease one-liners
  • Typhoid: Salmonella typhi, Widal test. Pneumonia: Streptococcus, alveoli fill with fluid. Common cold: rhinoviruses. Filariasis: Wuchereria via Culex. Ringworm: Microsporum/Trichophyton.
  • Cancer: benign (localised) vs malignant (metastasis); diagnosis by biopsy; treatment by surgery, radiotherapy, chemotherapy, immunotherapy.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

The infective stage of Plasmodium for humans and its vector are

Solution: Female Anopheles injects sporozoites with saliva; merozoites act inside RBCs and gametocytes infect the next mosquito.

Fig: Memory cells turn the second encounter into a rapid overwhelming response - the whole logic of vaccines and boosters.

Chapter 15 · Topic 1

Health

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Health (WHO) is complete physical, mental and social well-being - not merely absence of disease. It needs balanced diet, clean water, sanitation, exercise, vaccination and mental peace together; lacking any one breaks the whole. Public health therefore measures populations (IMR, MMR, life expectancy, disease burden), not just patients - prevention (hygiene, vaccines) beats cure by orders of magnitude in lives per rupee.

Topic in Depth · Health

Health (WHO) means complete physical, mental and social well-being - not merely disease absence. It needs balanced diet, clean water, sanitation, exercise, vaccination, mental peace and social support together; lacking any one breaks the whole. Public health measures populations (IMR, MMR, life expectancy, disease burden), not just patients - prevention (hygiene, vaccines) beats cure by orders of magnitude in lives per rupee. Health education multiplies every other intervention.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Physical plus mental plus social well-being = health.
  • Prevention beats cure in lives saved per rupee.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Health according to WHO is

Solution: WHO defines health positively and three-dimensionally, not as mere disease absence.

Chapter 15 · Topic 2

Diseases

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Health = complete physical, mental and social well-being (not mere absence of disease). Infectious diseases spread via pathogens — bacteria, viruses, protozoa, helminths, fungi, prions. Key examples: bacterial — typhoid (Salmonella typhi, Widal test), pneumonia (Streptococcus pneumoniae — alveoli fill with fluid), plague, diphtheria, tetanus; viral — common cold (rhinoviruses), influenza, dengue/chikungunya (Aedes mosquito); protozoan — malaria (Plasmodium; malignant tertian = P. falciparum), amoebiasis (Entamoeba histolytica); helminthic — ascariasis (Ascaris), filariasis/elephantiasis (Wuchereria, Culex vector); fungal — ringworm (Microsporum, Trichophyton).
NEET facts:
  • Malaria's infective stage for humans: sporozoites (in mosquito saliva).
  • Typhoid confirms with the Widal test; pneumonia affects the alveoli.

Deeper Understanding · Incubation logic + vectors table

Every pathogen has a characteristic incubation period (exposure to symptoms): days for cold/flu, weeks for typhoid, months for hepatitis, years for HIV/AIDS and rabies - quarantine lengths derive from these windows. Vector map: Anopheles (malaria), Aedes (dengue, chikungunya), Culex (filariasis); houseflies mechanically carry typhoid/cholera without being biological hosts. Bacterial diseases respond to antibiotics, viral ones never do - prescribing antibiotics for flu only breeds resistance, a public-health trap.

Topic in Depth · Diseases

Diseases split by cause and spread: infectious (pathogen-transmitted - bacterial, viral, protozoan, helminthic, fungal) versus non-infectious (deficiency, allergy, cancer, degeneration - never transmitted); acute (sudden, short - cold, typhoid) versus chronic (long-developing - TB, diabetes, arthritis); communicable (person-to-person) versus non-communicable. Classification decides response - antibiotics for bacterial infections, lifestyle for chronic diseases, quarantine for communicable outbreaks.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Infectious vs non-infectious; acute vs chronic; communicable vs not.
  • Cause classification decides treatment category.
Remember
  • Cancer and allergy are non-infectious - never transmitted between people.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Which disease is non-infectious?

Solution: Cancer arises from internal mutations, never from transmitted pathogens.

Chapter 15 · Topic 3

Pathogens

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Pathogens by type: bacteria (single-celled, antibiotic-sensitive), viruses (acellular, hijack host machinery), protozoa (animal-like protists), helminths (parasitic worms), fungi (skin specialists), prions (misfolded proteins, e.g. mad-cow). Transmission routes - air, water, food, vectors, sex, blood - decide control strategy: clean water kills cholera, condoms block HIV, mosquitoes targeted for malaria. Koch postulates formally prove a microbe causes a disease.

Topic in Depth · Pathogens

Pathogens span kingdoms and sizes: bacteria (single cells, antibiotic-sensitive), viruses (acellular, hijack host machinery - antibiotics useless), protozoa (animal-like protists), helminths (parasitic worms), fungi (skin specialists), prions (misfolded proteins, mad-cow). Transmission routes - air, water, food, vectors, sex, blood - decide control: clean water kills cholera, condoms block HIV, mosquitoes targeted for malaria. Koch postulates formally prove a microbe causes a disease (isolate, infect, re-isolate).
NEET Snapshot:
  • Bacteria, viruses, protozoa, helminths, fungi, prions - six pathogen types.
  • Route decides control: water, vectors, sex, blood.
Route control
PointDetail
AirborneMasks, ventilation (cold, flu, TB)
Water/foodSanitation (cholera, typhoid)
VectorMosquito control (malaria, dengue)
Sex/bloodCondoms, sterile needles (HIV)
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Koch postulates are used to

Solution: Isolate from patient, infect healthy host, re-isolate same microbe - causation proof.

Chapter 15 · Topic 4

Bacterial diseases

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Bacterial diseases: typhoid (Salmonella typhi - contaminated food/water, Widal test), pneumonia (Streptococcus pneumoniae - alveoli fill with fluid), plague (Yersinia, rat fleas), diphtheria (throat membrane), tetanus (Clostridium, lockjaw - vaccine preventable), tuberculosis (Mycobacterium, lungs). Antibiotics cure bacterial infections but never viral ones - completing full courses prevents resistance breeding.

Topic in Depth · Bacterial diseases

Bacterial diseases yield to antibiotics because bacteria are independent cells with unique targets (walls, ribosomes): typhoid (Salmonella typhi - contaminated food/water, Widal test), pneumonia (Streptococcus - alveoli fill with fluid), plague (Yersinia via rat fleas), diphtheria (throat membrane), tetanus (Clostridium lockjaw - vaccine preventable), TB (Mycobacterium chronic granulomas). Completing full antibiotic courses prevents resistance breeding - stopped-early survivors evolve into superbugs.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Antibiotics work (bacteria are independent cells with unique targets).
  • Typhoid-Widal, pneumonia-alveoli, tetanus-vaccine triads.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Widal test confirms

Solution: Antibody agglutination against Salmonella antigens diagnoses typhoid.

Chapter 15 · Topic 5

Viral diseases

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Viral diseases: common cold (rhinoviruses - most frequent human infection), influenza (shifting strains need yearly vaccines), dengue/chikungunya (Aedes mosquito, breakbone fever), hepatitis (liver, A-E types), rabies (almost always fatal once symptomatic - immediate vaccine saves), AIDS (retrovirus, lifelong management). Treatment is mostly supportive + vaccination; antibiotics are useless and their misuse only breeds resistance.

Topic in Depth · Viral diseases

Viral diseases defeat antibiotics (viruses hijack host machinery with few unique targets): common cold (rhinoviruses - most frequent human infection, no cure, only relief), influenza (shifting strains need yearly vaccines), dengue/chikungunya (Aedes mosquito, breakbone fever, platelet crash), hepatitis (liver A-E types, vaccine for A/B), rabies (near-certain fatality once symptomatic - immediate post-bite vaccine saves), AIDS (lifelong retroviral management). Prevention is vaccination plus vectors; treatment mostly supportive.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Antibiotics never work on viruses - different biology entirely.
  • Rabies: vaccine window closes once symptoms start.
Remember
  • Dengue drops platelets; rabies kills after symptoms; flu shifts yearly.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Dengue spreads through bite of

Solution: Day-biting Aedes carries dengue and chikungunya; Anopheles carries malaria.

Chapter 15 · Topic 6

Protozoan diseases

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Protozoan diseases: malaria (Plasmodium - Anopheles vector, haemozoin fevers), amoebiasis (Entamoeba histolytica - contaminated water, dysentery with blood/mucus), sleeping sickness (Trypanosoma - tsetse fly), kala-azar (Leishmania - sandfly), giardiasis. Diagnosis often needs blood smears or stool microscopy; prevention targets vectors plus clean water - the two classic tropical-medicine levers.

Topic in Depth · Protozoan diseases

Protozoan diseases ride vectors and water: malaria (Plasmodium - Anopheles, haemozoin fevers), amoebiasis (Entamoeba - contaminated water, bloody dysentery), sleeping sickness (Trypanosoma - tsetse fly, neurological), kala-azar (Leishmania - sandfly, spleen-liver enlargement), giardiasis (contaminated water, fatty diarrhoea). Diagnosis needs blood smears or stool microscopy spotting the parasites; prevention splits between vector control and clean water - the two tropical-medicine levers.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Vectors (flies, mosquitoes) plus water carry protozoan diseases.
  • Smears and stool tests spot the parasites directly.

Solution: Leishmania rides sandflies to spleen and liver - visceral leishmaniasis.

Chapter 15 · Topic 7

Helminthic diseases

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Helminthic diseases: ascariasis (Ascaris - contaminated food, wandering larvae damage lungs), filariasis/elephantiasis (Wuchereria - Culex vector, lymph blockage swells limbs), hookworm (Ancylostoma - skin penetration, anaemia), tapeworm (Taenia - undercooked pork/beef, proglottids in stool), pinworm (childhood itching). Deworming tablets plus sanitation break faecal-oral and vector cycles together.

Topic in Depth · Helminthic diseases

Helminthic diseases come from parasitic worms via food, skin or vectors: ascariasis (Ascaris eggs in food - larval lung migration then intestinal adults), filariasis (Wuchereria via Culex - lymph blockage swelling), hookworm (Ancylostoma skin penetration - blood-sucking anaemia), tapeworm (Taenia undercooked meat - proglottids in stool), pinworm (childhood itching, easy spread). Deworming tablets (albendazole) plus sanitation plus footwear break faecal-oral, skin and vector cycles together.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Food (Ascaris, Taenia), skin (hookworm), vectors (Wuchereria) - three entries.
  • Deworming plus sanitation plus footwear breaks cycles.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Hookworm enters human body through

Solution: Ancylostoma larvae bore through bare foot skin to reach intestines.

Chapter 15 · Topic 8

Malaria

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Malaria (Plasmodium vivax/ovale - benign tertian; malariae - quartan; falciparum - malignant tertian, most dangerous): female Anopheles injects sporozoites, liver multiplication, then RBC cycles releasing toxic haemozoin with periodic chills-fever-sweat. Gametocytes continue the cycle in the next mosquito. Control: drainage (no stagnant water), larvivorous fish (Gambusia), bed nets, quinine/chloroquine/artemisinin drugs - resistance demands combination therapy.

Topic in Depth · Malaria

Malaria runs a two-host cycle: female Anopheles injects sporozoites (infective stage) with saliva; liver multiplication releases merozoites invading RBCs, digesting haemoglobin and spilling toxic haemozoin with periodic chill-fever-sweat; some merozoites become gametocytes continuing in the next mosquito gut (sexual phase, sporogony). Species grade danger: falciparum (malignant tertian, most lethal), vivax/ovale (benign tertian), malariae (quartan). Control layers drainage, Gambusia fish, bed nets and artemisinin combination drugs against resistance.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Sporozoites in, haemozoin fevers, gametocytes out - two hosts.
  • Falciparum malignant tertian kills; others debilitate.
Malaria types
PointDetail
Vivax/ovaleBenign tertian (48-hour)
MalariaeQuartan (72-hour)
FalciparumMalignant tertian (irregular, lethal)

1

Haemozoin (malaria pigment) is released when

Solution: RBC rupture spills haemozoin toxin - triggering the classic fever paroxysm.

Chapter 15 · Topic 9

Typhoid

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Typhoid (Salmonella typhi): spreads via contaminated food and water (houseflies as mechanical carriers); bacteria invade Peyer patches of the small intestine, then bloodstream - sustained high fever, headache, abdominal pain, rose spots. Confirmed by blood culture early and the Widal test (antibody agglutination) later. Vaccines (Typhi Vi) plus safe water prevent it; Mary Mallon (Typhoid Mary) proved healthy carriers spread disease silently.

Topic in Depth · Typhoid

Typhoid (Salmonella typhi) invades via contaminated food and water (houseflies as mechanical carriers): bacteria breach Peyer patches of the small intestine into blood - sustained high fever, headache, abdominal pain, rose spots, coated tongue. Blood culture confirms early; Widal test (antibody agglutination) confirms later. Vaccines (Typhi Vi) plus safe water prevent it; carriers like Typhoid Mary (healthy, shedding) prove silent spread - hygiene plus vaccination beats treatment alone.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Faecal-oral spread; Peyer patches entry; sustained fever signature.
  • Blood culture early, Widal later; carriers spread silently.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Typhoid spreads mainly through

Solution: Salmonella rides faecal-oral routes - sanitation plus vaccination prevents.

Chapter 15 · Topic 10

Pneumonia

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Pneumonia (Streptococcus pneumoniae, Haemophilus): alveoli fill with fluid and pus instead of air - fever, chills, cough with rusty sputum, breathing difficulty. Chest X-ray shows consolidation; vaccines (pneumococcal) protect the vulnerable. It differs from bronchitis (airway inflammation) and TB (chronic granulomas) - location plus pathogen define each respiratory disease precisely.

Topic in Depth · Pneumonia

Pneumonia (Streptococcus pneumoniae, Haemophilus) fills alveoli with fluid and pus instead of air - fever, chills, cough with rusty sputum, breathing difficulty, chest pain. X-ray shows consolidation shadows; pneumococcal vaccines protect infants plus elderly. It differs from bronchitis (airway inflammation, wheezing) and TB (chronic granulomas, night sweats, weight loss) - location plus pathogen plus course define each respiratory disease precisely for diagnosis questions.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Alveoli fill with fluid - gas exchange drowns.
  • X-ray consolidation plus rusty sputum diagnose.
Remember
  • Bronchitis wheezes (airways), pneumonia consolidates (alveoli), TB wastes (chronic).
NEET Practice MCQ
1

In pneumonia, fluid accumulates in the

Solution: Infected alveoli fill with exudate, blocking gas exchange at the surface.

Chapter 15 · Topic 11

Amoebiasis

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Amoebiasis (Entamoeba histolytica): cysts in contaminated food/water hatch in the intestine; trophozoites invade the colon wall causing dysentery with blood and mucus, sometimes liver abscesses. Diagnosis by stool microscopy (cysts/trophozoites with ingested RBCs). Metronidazole cures it; boiling water and hygienic food prevent it - the classic faecal-oral disease of unsafe sanitation.

Topic in Depth · Amoebiasis

Amoebiasis (Entamoeba histolytica) starts as swallowed cysts in contaminated food/water hatching in the intestine, where trophozoites invade the colon wall causing dysentery with blood and mucus - flask-shaped ulcers pathognomonic - sometimes reaching the liver as abscesses. Stool microscopy (cysts, trophozoites with ingested RBCs) diagnoses; metronidazole cures. Boiling water plus hygienic food prevents the faecal-oral cycle - the classic unsafe-sanitation disease.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Cysts in, trophozoites invade colon, blood-mucus dysentery out.
  • Liver abscess is the dangerous extra-intestinal spread.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Amoebic dysentery is characterised by stools with

Solution: Invaded colon wall bleeds and secretes mucus - distinguishing amoebic from viral diarrhoea.

Chapter 15 · Topic 12

Ascariasis

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Ascariasis (Ascaris lumbricoides): swallowed eggs hatch in the gut; larvae migrate gut-liver-heart-lungs (causing cough), get swallowed again, and mature into large intestinal worms competing for nutrition - heavy loads block intestines and stunt children. Females lay ~200,000 eggs daily, surviving years in soil. Single-dose albendazole/mebendazole plus handwashing and sanitation break the cycle.

Topic in Depth · Ascariasis

Ascariasis (Ascaris lumbricoides) runs a body tour: swallowed eggs hatch in the gut, larvae migrate gut-liver-heart-lungs (causing cough and infiltrates), get coughed up and swallowed again, maturing into large intestinal adults competing for nutrition - heavy loads block intestines and stunt children. Females lay ~200,000 eggs daily surviving years in soil. Single-dose albendazole/mebendazole plus handwashing, footwear and sanitation break the faecal-oral loop.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Egg to migrating larva to gut adult - tour before settling.
  • 200,000 eggs daily per female - soil stays infectious for years.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Ascariasis spreads through

Solution: Faecal-oral egg ingestion starts the migratory worm cycle.

Chapter 15 · Topic 13

Filariasis

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Filariasis/elephantiasis (Wuchereria bancrofti): Culex mosquito deposits infective larvae that mature in lymph vessels, blocking drainage - gross limb/genital swelling develops over years. Night blood smears reveal microfilariae (they circulate nocturnally with mosquito biting). DEC drug kills microfilariae; mosquito control plus hygiene prevents progression - early treatment reverses what late disease makes permanent.

Topic in Depth · Filariasis

Filariasis (Wuchereria bancrofti) blocks lymphatics: Culex mosquito deposits infective larvae maturing in lymph vessels, chronic inflammation plus fibrosis obstructs drainage - gross limb/genital swelling (elephantiasis) develops over years. Night blood smears reveal microfilariae (nocturnal circulation matching mosquito biting). DEC kills microfilariae; mosquito control plus hygiene prevents progression - early treatment reverses what late fibrosis makes permanent.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Culex deposits larvae; lymph blockage swells limbs over years.
  • Night smears catch nocturnal microfilariae.
Remember
  • Chronicity defines filariasis - years of infection precede swelling.

1

Elephantiasis vector is

Solution: Culex mosquitoes carry Wuchereria larvae to lymphatic vessels.

Chapter 15 · Topic 14

Immunity

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Innate (inborn, non-specific): physical (skin, mucus), physiological (HCl, lysozyme, interferons), cellular (phagocytes like neutrophils/macrophages), inflammatory and fever responses. Acquired (specific): active — after infection or vaccination (slow start, lasting memory cells); passive — ready-made antibodies (mother's IgA in colostrum, antivenom, anti-tetanus serum). B-lymphocytes → humoral immunity (antibodies); T-lymphocytes → cell-mediated immunity (helper T cells coordinate; killer T cells destroy).
Fig: Antibody (H₂L₂) — two heavy + two light chains; tips bind specific antigens. Classes: IgG, IgA, IgM, IgE, IgD.

Deeper Understanding · Clonal selection in one paragraph

Each lymphocyte is pre-committed to one antigen shape; when its match arrives, that clone alone multiplies into an army (plasma cells pumping antibodies + memory cells waiting years). This clonal selection explains vaccination (practice invasion builds memory), booster doses (memory expands faster and stronger), and why newborns respond poorly (naive repertoire, maternal IgA covers the gap). Autoimmunity is clonal selection misfiring - a forbidden self-reactive clone escapes deletion (rheumatoid arthritis, myasthenia gravis, Hashimoto thyroiditis).

Topic in Depth · Immunity

Immunity layers defence: innate (inborn, non-specific - skin, mucus, HCl, lysozyme, interferons, phagocytes, fever, inflammation) responds instantly to everything; acquired (specific) responds slowly first time but remembers - active (infection or vaccination, lasting memory) versus passive (ready-made antibodies: maternal IgA colostrum, antivenom - temporary). B-cells run humoral antibody warfare; T-cells run cell-mediated killing and coordination (helper CD4 conductors, killer CD8 executors). Vaccination is safe active immunity rehearsal.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Innate instant-general; acquired slow-specific with memory.
  • B humoral, T cellular; active lasts, passive fades.
Immunity grid
PointDetail
Active naturalInfection survived, memory kept
Active artificialVaccination, memory built safely
Passive naturalMaternal IgA via colostrum
Passive artificialAntivenom, antisera - instant, brief
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Colostrum provides which immunity to newborns?

Solution: Ready-made maternal IgA protects until the infant own system matures.
Back to Index
Index / Health & Welfare
Chapter 15 · Topic 15

Antibodies

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Antibodies (immunoglobulins, H2L2): two heavy + two light chains with variable antigen-binding tips and constant tails, linked by disulphide bonds. Classes: IgG (most abundant, crosses placenta), IgA (colostrum, mucosa), IgM (first responder, largest), IgE (allergy), IgD (B-cell receptor). Monoclonal antibodies (hybridoma technology) now target cancers and autoimmune diseases with sniper precision.

Topic in Depth · Antibodies

Antibodies (immunoglobulins, H2L2) pair two heavy plus two light chains with variable antigen-gripping tips and constant tails, linked by disulphide bonds. Classes specialise: IgG (most abundant, crosses placenta), IgA (colostrum, mucosa), IgM (first responder, largest pentamer), IgE (allergy trigger), IgD (B-cell receptor). Monoclonal antibodies (hybridoma clones) now target cancers and autoimmune diseases with sniper precision - diagnosis plus therapy from one molecule family.
NEET Snapshot:
  • H2L2 with variable tips (grip) and constant tails (signal).
  • IgG abundant, IgA mucosal, IgM first, IgE allergy.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Antibodies crossing the placenta are mainly

Solution: Maternal IgG crosses to shield newborns systemically before their own synthesis.
Back to Index
Index / Health & Welfare
Chapter 15 · Topic 16

Vaccination

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Vaccination exposes the body to dead/weakened pathogens so memory cells form — active immunity without disease (smallpox eradicated this way). Allergy: exaggerated response to harmless antigens (dust, pollen) — IgE triggers mast cells to release histamine and serotonin (sneezing, hives); antihistamines treat it. Autoimmunity: immunity attacks self — e.g. rheumatoid arthritis, myasthenia gravis, Hashimoto's thyroiditis.

Why do allergy medicines target histamine, and which antibody starts the reaction?

Answer: IgE-bound mast cells dump histamine causing allergy symptoms; antihistamines block its receptors and calm the response.

Deeper Understanding · Anaphylaxis - allergy gone systemic

Local allergy (sneezing, hives) becomes anaphylaxis when mediator release goes body-wide: vessels dilate, BP crashes, airways swell shut within minutes of a sting, peanut or penicillin dose. Adrenaline injection is the antidote (constricts vessels, opens airways, boosts heart) - antihistamines are too slow for emergencies. Atopy (allergic tendency) runs in families with high IgE; desensitisation therapy retrains the system with graded allergen doses. Hygiene hypothesis links cleaner childhoods to rising allergy rates.

Topic in Depth · Vaccination

Vaccination stages safe practice invasions: killed, weakened or subunit pathogens (plus toxoids for diphtheria-tetanus) trigger active immunity with memory cells minus disease risk - smallpox eradicated, polio nearly so. Boosters refresh fading memory (tetanus decennially); schedules front-load childhood (BCG, DPT, OPV, MMR). Herd immunity (high coverage) shields the unvaccinated few - falling coverage revives measles outbreaks, the visible cost of hesitancy.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Safe rehearsal building real memory - smallpox proof of principle.
  • Boosters refresh; herd coverage shields the vulnerable.
Remember
  • Live vaccines (OPV, MMR) beat killed ones for strength, not safety profile.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Smallpox was eradicated globally mainly through

Solution: Vaccinia vaccination broke transmission chains until zero natural cases remained.

Index / Health & Welfare
Chapter 15 · Topic 17

AIDS

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
HIV (retrovirus: RNA + reverse transcriptase) transmitted via unprotected sex, infected blood/needles and placenta — not by touch or shared food. It destroys helper T-lymphocytes (CD4), collapsing immunity; the patient dies of opportunistic infections. Diagnosis by ELISA; anti-retroviral therapy extends life but no cure or vaccine exists. NACO spreads awareness in India.
Common mistakes: HIV is a retrovirus (RNA), not a DNA virus; its enzyme reverse transcriptase makes DNA from RNA.

Deeper Understanding · NACO strategy + safe blood

India NACO fights HIV on three fronts: awareness (schools, red ribbon), safe blood (mandatory HIV/HBV screening of every unit, single-use needles), and care (free anti-retroviral therapy prolonging productive life). Mother-to-child transmission is cut with drugs plus advised delivery practices. Because no vaccine or cure exists, behaviour remains the only shield - which is why the chapter stresses what does NOT spread HIV (touch, hugging, shared food, mosquito bites) as heavily as what does.

Related Diagram · HIV retrovirus

Fig: Spikes find CD4 cells, RNA becomes DNA inside them, immunity collapses from within.

Topic in Depth · AIDS

AIDS is end-stage HIV retrovirus infection: transmitted via unprotected sex, infected blood/needles and placenta (never touch, food or mosquitoes), HIV integrates proviral DNA into CD4 helper T-cells and hides latently for years, then destroys coordination - opportunistic infections and cancers kill, not the virus directly. ELISA screens antibodies (window period blind early); anti-retroviral therapy suppresses but never cures. NACO fights via awareness, safe blood and free treatment - behaviour remains the only shield.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Retrovirus kills CD4 helpers; opportunists finish the job.
  • Window period blinds tests early; no cure or vaccine exists.

ControlAnti-retrovirals + behaviour

HIV destroys primarily

Solution: Losing immune conductors collapses coordinated defence against everything.

Chapter 15 · Topic 18

Cancer

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Uncontrolled cell division forms tumours: benign (localised, slow) vs malignant (invasive + metastasis through blood/lymph). Causes: physical (radiations), chemical (in tobacco smoke) and biological (oncogenic viruses, e.g. HPV) carcinogens; cellular oncogenes and lost tumour-suppressor genes. Diagnosis: biopsy, imaging, tumour markers; treatment: surgery, radiotherapy, chemotherapy, immunotherapy.

Deeper Understanding · Oncogenes vs suppressors

Normal cells divide on orders: proto-oncogenes say go (growth factors), tumour suppressors say stop (p53 - the guardian of the genome, BRCA1/2 in breast-ovarian families). Cancer needs both failures - stuck accelerator plus cut brakes - usually across years of mutations, which is why most cancers are age-related. Metastasis (travel through blood/lymph to seed new tumours) is what makes malignancy lethal, not the primary lump. Tumour markers (PSA, CA-125) and imaging catch it early; surgery, radiotherapy, chemotherapy and immunotherapy attack it four different ways.

Related Diagram · Benign vs malignant tumour

Fig: Benign lumps stay wrapped; malignant cells voyage through blood and lymph to seed new tumours.

Topic in Depth · Cancer

Cancer is uncontrolled division plus invasion: benign tumours stay capsulated and local; malignant cancers invade plus metastasise through blood/lymph to seed distant tumours - metastasis is what kills. Causes span physical (radiation), chemical (tobacco carcinogens), biological (HPV, hepatitis viruses) plus inherited mutations (BRCA) - cellularly, stuck accelerators (oncogenes) plus cut brakes (tumour suppressors like p53). Diagnosis by biopsy, imaging, markers (PSA); treatment by surgery, radiotherapy, chemotherapy, immunotherapy combined.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Benign localised; malignant invasive plus metastatic.
  • Oncogenes accelerate; suppressor loss removes brakes.
Remember
  • Metastasis, not the primary lump, causes most cancer deaths.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Metastasis refers to

Solution: Travelling seeds founding new tumours define malignancy lethality.

Chapter 15 · Topic 19

Allergy

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Allergy is immunity overreacting to harmless antigens (dust, pollen, peanuts): IgE arms mast cells, which dump histamine + serotonin - sneezing, hives, constricted airways. Antihistamines block mild cases; adrenaline reverses systemic anaphylaxis (crashing BP, closing throat) within minutes. Atopy runs in families; desensitisation shots retrain tolerance with graded doses.

Topic in Depth · Allergy

Allergy is immunity overreacting to harmless antigens (dust, pollen, peanuts, penicillin): IgE arms mast cells and basophils, which dump histamine plus serotonin - sneezing, hives, constricted airways. Antihistamines block mild cases; adrenaline reverses systemic anaphylaxis (crashing BP, closing throat) within minutes. Atopy runs in families with high IgE; desensitisation shots retrain tolerance with graded doses. Hygiene hypothesis links cleaner childhoods to rising allergy rates.
NEET Snapshot:
  • IgE plus mast-cell histamine = allergy chemistry.
  • Antihistamines for hives; adrenaline for anaphylaxis.

Histamine in allergy is released mainly from

Solution: IgE-triggered degranulation floods tissues with histamine and serotonin.

Chapter 15 · Topic 20

Autoimmune diseases

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Autoimmune diseases attack self when forbidden lymphocyte clones escape deletion: rheumatoid arthritis (joints), myasthenia gravis (muscle receptors), Hashimoto thyroiditis (thyroid), type-1 diabetes (pancreatic beta cells), multiple sclerosis (myelin), lupus (systemic). Treatment suppresses immunity (steroids, monoclonals) - trading infection risk for self-tolerance. Molecular mimicry (pathogen resembling self) often triggers the original mistake.

Topic in Depth · Autoimmune diseases

Autoimmune diseases attack self when forbidden lymphocyte clones escape deletion: rheumatoid arthritis (joints), myasthenia gravis (muscle receptors), Hashimoto thyroiditis (thyroid), type-1 diabetes (pancreatic beta cells), multiple sclerosis (myelin), lupus (systemic multi-organ). Molecular mimicry (pathogen resembling self) often triggers the original mistake. Treatment suppresses immunity (steroids, monoclonals) - trading infection risk for self-tolerance, managed lifelong.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Self-reactive clones escape deletion and attack own tissues.
  • Mimicry triggers; immunosuppression manages.

1

Myasthenia gravis is

Solution: Antibodies destroy acetylcholine receptors - signals arrive but muscles cannot listen.

Chapter 15 · Topic 21

Drug and alcohol abuse

Human Health and DiseaseHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Heroin/smack (poppy — depressant, injected/snorted), cocaine/crack (Erythroxylum coca — stimulates dopamine, hallucinations), cannabinoids (Cannabis — marijuana, hashish, charas, ganja; affect heart), morphine (medical sedative, abused), tobacco (chewing → oral cancer; smoking → lung/bladder cancer, emphysema). Alcohol abuse damages the liver (cirrhosis), nerves and family life; withdrawal needs medical and psychological support.

Deeper Understanding · Dependence vs withdrawal

Addictive drugs hijack the dopamine reward circuit: cocaine blocks dopamine reuptake (stimulation), heroin mimics endorphins (depression of pain), cannabinoids distort perception, tobacco nicotine sharpens then hooks. Tolerance forces bigger doses; withdrawal (tremors, nausea, anxiety, seizures in alcohol) forces continued use - dependence is therefore both chemical and learned. Adolescent brains are most vulnerable (reward circuits mature before self-control circuits); peer pressure, exam stress and curiosity are the classic entry points prevention targets.

Topic in Depth · Drug and alcohol abuse

Addictive drugs hijack the dopamine reward circuit: heroin/smack (poppy depressant), cocaine/crack (Erythroxylum stimulant with hallucinations), cannabinoids (marijuana, hashish, charas - heart effects), morphine (medical sedative abused), tobacco (chewing causes oral cancer; smoking causes lung cancer, emphysema). Alcohol damages liver (cirrhosis), nerves and families. Tolerance forces bigger doses; withdrawal (tremors to seizures) forces continued use - adolescent brains (mature reward, immature control) are most vulnerable; peers plus stress open the door.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Dependence is chemical (tolerance, withdrawal) plus learned.
  • Tobacco-chewing: oral cancer. Smoking: lung cancer plus emphysema.

Chapter 16 · Topic 1

Microbes

Microbes in Human WelfareHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Microbes (bacteria, fungi, viruses, protozoa) run the planet: decomposers recycling nutrients, nitrogen fixers feeding plants, gut flora digesting food - pathogens are a tiny minority. Humans harness them for fermented foods, antibiotics, enzymes, acids, biofuels, sewage cleaning, biogas and biofertilisers - the same metabolism that spoils milk makes cheese, and the same growth that causes disease makes penicillin. This chapter is the useful side of microbiology.

Topic in Depth · Microbes

Microbes (bacteria, fungi, viruses, protozoa) run planetary cycles - decomposers recycling nutrients, nitrogen fixers feeding plants, gut flora digesting food - with pathogens a tiny minority. Humans harness their metabolism for fermented foods, antibiotics, enzymes, acids, biofuels, sewage cleaning, biogas and biofertilisers: the same growth that spoils milk makes cheese, and the same chemistry that causes disease makes penicillin. This chapter is microbiology useful side - every process here earns its keep.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Decomposers, fixers, fermenters - pathogens are the minority.
  • Same metabolism spoils and serves - context decides value.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Which statement about microbes is correct?

Solution: Pathogens are a small fraction; decomposers, fermenters and fixers sustain life.

Chapter 16 · Topic 2

Fermentation

Microbes in Human WelfareHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Fermentation is anaerobic sugar breakdown by microbes: Saccharomyces yeast converts malted cereals/fruit juices to ethanol + CO2 - wine and beer stay undistilled (under ~15% alcohol, yeast poison limit), while whisky, rum and vodka are distilled concentrates. Lactic-acid bacteria curdle milk to curd, yoghurt and cheese; dough rises on yeast CO2. The same pathway powers muscle sprinting (lactic acid) when oxygen runs short.

Related Diagram · Yeast fermentation

Fig: Sugar plus yeast minus oxygen: alcohol for brewers, carbon dioxide for bakers.

Topic in Depth · Fermentation

Fermentation is anaerobic sugar breakdown: Saccharomyces yeast converts malted cereals and fruit juices to ethanol plus CO2 - wine and beer stay undistilled (under ~15 percent alcohol, yeast poison limit), while whisky, rum and vodka distil concentrates (~40 percent plus). Lactic-acid bacteria curdle milk to curd, yoghurt and cheese; dough rises on yeast CO2; muscles sprint on the same lactic pathway when oxygen runs short. One chemistry serves brewers, bakers and sprinters.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Yeast ceiling ~15 percent; distillation concentrates beyond.
  • Wine/beer undistilled; whisky/rum/vodka distilled.
Remember
  • Dough rise and sprint burn share lactic/CO2 chemistry with brewing.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Beer and wine remain low-alcohol because they are

Solution: Yeast die in their own alcohol around 15 percent; distillation alone goes higher.

Chapter 16 · Topic 3

Antibiotics

Microbes in Human WelfareHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Antibiotics kill or stall bacteria by attacking bacteria-only targets: penicillin (Penicillium notatum - Fleming, Florey, Chain, Nobel) blocks cell-wall synthesis - safe for wall-less human cells. Others block ribosomes (tetracycline), DNA copying or folate synthesis. Misuse breeds resistance (beta-lactamase enzymes, efflux pumps) - complete prescribed courses, never self-medicate, and never use them for viral colds.

Topic in Depth · Antibiotics

Antibiotics kill or stall bacteria by attacking bacteria-only targets: penicillin (Penicillium notatum - Fleming, Florey, Chain, Nobel) blocks cell-wall synthesis - safe for wall-less human cells; others jam ribosomes (tetracycline), DNA copying or folate synthesis. Misuse breeds resistance (beta-lactamase enzymes, efflux pumps) - complete prescribed courses, never self-medicate, never use them for viral colds. Each unfinished course trains survivors.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Attack bacteria-only targets; spare human cells by design.
  • Finish courses - survivors of half-doses evolve resistance.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Penicillin acts by blocking bacterial

Solution: Wall-less human cells escape - selective toxicity by structural difference.

Chapter 16 · Topic 4

Industrial products

Microbes in Human WelfareHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Fermented beverages: yeast (Saccharomyces) ferments malted cereals/fruit juices — wine/beer undistilled; whisky/rum/vodka distilled. Antibiotics: penicillin (Penicillium notatum — Fleming, Florey, Chain) kills bacteria by blocking wall synthesis. Organic acids: citric acid (Aspergillus niger), acetic acid (Acetobacter aceti), butyric acid (Clostridium butylicum), lactic acid (Lactobacillus). Enzymes: lipase (laundry detergents), pectinase/protease (juice clarification), streptokinase (Streptococcus — clot buster in heart patients). Statins (Monascus purpureus yeast) lower blood cholesterol.
NEET facts:
  • First antibiotic: penicillin; Fleming–Florey–Chain won the Nobel for it.
  • Bottled fruit juices are clarified with pectinases and proteases.

Deeper Understanding · Distilled vs undistilled + antibiotics logic

Fermentation makes at most ~15% alcohol (yeast poison themselves); distillation concentrates it into whisky, rum, vodka (~40%+), while wine and beer stay undistilled. Antibiotics exploit bacteria-only targets - penicillin blocks cell-wall synthesis (human cells have no walls, hence selective safety). Overuse breeds resistance (beta-lactamase enzymes, efflux pumps), so complete prescribed courses and no self-medication are public-health duties, not suggestions. Streptokinase dissolves clots after heart attacks; cyclosporin (fungus) prevents transplant rejection.

Topic in Depth · Industrial products

Industrial microbiology scales microbe chemistry: fermented beverages (yeast ethanol), antibiotics (penicillin first), organic acids (citric-Aspergillus niger, acetic-Acetobacter, butyric-Clostridium, lactic-Lactobacillus), enzymes (lipase detergents, pectinase juice clarification), plus streptokinase clot-busters and statin cholesterol-blockers. Fermenters control pH, aeration and sterility to turn vats into factories - the same species that spoil food, domesticated for production.
NEET Snapshot:
  • One microbe per product; conditions tuned per vat.
  • Acids, enzymes, drugs - microbe factories span industries.
Product makers
PointDetail

Acetic acidAcetobacter aceti
Butyric acidClostridium butylicum
Lactic acidLactobacillus

Chapter 16 · Topic 5

Enzymes

Microbes in Human WelfareHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Industrial enzymes: lipases in laundry detergents (dissolve grease stains), pectinases + proteases clarify bottled juices, amylases process starch syrups, streptokinase (Streptococcus) dissolves clots in heart-attack patients. Immobilised enzymes on beads work repeatedly in bioreactors - the same specificity that runs cells now runs factories, gently and without harsh chemistry.

Topic in Depth · Enzymes

Industrial enzymes deploy protein specificity in factories: lipases in detergents dissolve grease stains at low temperatures, pectinases plus proteases clarify bottled juices, amylases process starch syrups, streptokinase (Streptococcus) dissolves heart-attack clots in patients. Immobilised enzymes on beads work repeatedly in bioreactors - gentle, specific, biodegradable chemistry replacing harsh industrial reagents. Same catalytic precision that runs cells now runs production lines.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Specific, gentle, reusable (immobilised) factory catalysts.
  • Streptokinase doubles as life-saving clot-buster drug.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Bottled fruit juices are clarified using

Solution: Pectin plus protein haze digests away, leaving clear juice.

Chapter 16 · Topic 6

Bioactive molecules

Microbes in Human WelfareHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Bioactive molecules from microbes double as medicines: statins (Monascus purpureus yeast) block cholesterol synthesis, cyclosporin (fungus) suppresses transplant rejection, streptokinase busts clots. Screening soil microbes for such molecules built the modern drug cabinet - each compound a microbial chemical weapon repurposed for human therapy. Statins remain among the most prescribed drugs worldwide.

Topic in Depth · Bioactive molecules

Bioactive molecules are microbial chemical weapons repurposed as medicines: statins (Monascus purpureus yeast) block cholesterol synthesis (among the most prescribed drugs worldwide), cyclosporin (fungus) suppresses transplant rejection, streptokinase busts clots. Soil screening for such molecules built the modern drug cabinet - each compound evolved to kill competitors, refined to heal patients. Dose decides poison versus prescription.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Statins lower cholesterol; cyclosporin guards transplants.
  • Soil microbes remain the richest drug-discovery source.
Remember
  • Microbial warfare chemistry becomes human pharmacy.

Chapter 16 · Topic 7

Sewage treatment

Microbes in Human WelfareHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Primary treatment: filtration/sedimentation removes grit and solids. Secondary (biological): sewage pumped into aeration tanks where aerobic microbes form flocs (bacterial + fungal masses) that digest organics, sharply lowering BOD (biochemical oxygen demand); settled activated sludge partly recycled, rest sent to anaerobic sludge digesters where methanogens release biogas (CH₄-rich). Gobar-gas plants: cattle dung slurry fermented by Methanobacterium gives cooking fuel + spent slurry as fertiliser.

After secondary treatment the effluent's BOD has fallen sharply. What does low BOD signify?

Answer: Little biodegradable organic matter remains — microbes have consumed it, so the water is safe to discharge into rivers.

Common mistakes: BOD measures oxygen microbes consume, not oxygen dissolved in water (that's DO).

Deeper Understanding · BOD vs COD vs DO

Three oxygen letters, three meanings: DO (dissolved oxygen actually in water - fish need 5+ mg/L), BOD (oxygen microbes consume digesting organics - pollution load), COD (oxygen to chemically oxidise everything - always higher than BOD, faster to measure). Clean river: high DO, low BOD. Sewage inlet: low DO, high BOD. After secondary treatment: BOD crashes, DO recovers downstream - the oxygen sag curve maps this recovery stretch. Ganga Action Plan and Yamuna plans target exactly these numbers.

Topic in Depth · Sewage treatment

Sewage treatment stages physics then biology: primary filtration plus sedimentation removes grit and solids; secondary aeration tanks let aerobic microbes form flocs digesting organics - BOD (biochemical oxygen demand) crashes, signalling clean-enough effluent; settled activated sludge partly recycles, the rest digests anaerobically releasing biogas. Tertiary polishing (disinfection, nutrient removal) finishes where rivers demand it. The oxygen-sag curve maps river recovery downstream of outfalls.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Primary removes solids; secondary (flocs) cuts BOD; digesters make biogas.
  • Low BOD means little biodegradable matter remains.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Sharp BOD fall after secondary treatment means

Solution: Flocs consumed the organics - microbes have nothing left to demand oxygen for.

Chapter 16 · Topic 8

Biogas

Microbes in Human WelfareHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Biogas (gobar gas): cattle-dung slurry ferments anaerobically by Methanobacterium in dome digesters, releasing methane-rich gas for cooking/lighting plus spent slurry as nitrogen-rich fertiliser. Floating-drum (KVIC) and fixed-dome (Deenbandhu) designs fit villages; the system converts waste to wealth while cutting firewood smoke and indoor pollution - rural energy plus sanitation in one pit.

Topic in Depth · Biogas

Biogas (gobar gas) ferments cattle-dung slurry anaerobically via Methanobacterium in dome digesters - methane-rich cooking fuel plus spent slurry as nitrogen-rich fertiliser, two products from one waste. Floating-drum (KVIC) and fixed-dome (Deenbandhu) designs fit villages; the system cuts firewood smoke (indoor pollution, deforestation) while paying for sanitation. Waste to wealth plus health - rural energy with fertiliser change.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Dung plus methanogens minus oxygen equals fuel plus fertiliser.
  • Cuts smoke, saves trees, sanitises villages simultaneously.
Remember
  • Methanobacterium also bubbles marsh gas and cattle belches naturally.

Biogas is mainly

Chapter 16 · Topic 9

Biofertilisers

Microbes in Human WelfareHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Biocontrol: ladybird beetles and dragonflies eat pests; Bacillus thuringiensis (Bt) toxin kills caterpillars (engineered into Bt-cotton); NPV/baculoviruses attack insects genus-specifically; Trichoderma fungus suppresses plant pathogens. Biofertilisers: Rhizobium (legume root nodules fix N₂), Azospirillum/Azotobacter (free-living fixers), mycorrhiza (Glomus) supplies phosphorus, cyanobacteria like Anabaena/Nostoc enrich paddy fields, and Azolla fern hosts nitrogen-fixing Anabaena.
NEET facts:
  • Single-cell protein: Spirulina (250× more protein per area than conventional crops).
  • Mycorrhiza mainly helps plants absorb phosphorus.

Deeper Understanding · Bt genes by name + mycorrhiza math

Bt cotton carries bacterial cry genes (cryIAc, cryIIAb): alkaline insect guts dissolve the Cry protoxin into active toxin that punches holes in gut cells - caterpillars starve while humans (acidic guts, no receptors) stay safe. Refuge (non-Bt) strips slow resistance evolution. Mycorrhiza (Glomus) extends root absorption area up to 100-fold, mainly for phosphorus, trading plant sugars for minerals. Azolla-Anabaena partnership fixes so much nitrogen that paddy fields need less urea - the original green biofertiliser centuries before the term existed.

Related Diagram · Root nodule partnership

Fig: Bacteria get shelter plus food; legumes get usable nitrogen - fertiliser grown, not bought.

Topic in Depth · Biofertilisers

Biofertilisers are living nutrient suppliers: Rhizobium (legume root nodules fixing N2), Azospirillum/Azotobacter (free-living fixers), mycorrhiza Glomus (fungal phosphorus delivery across 100-fold root area), cyanobacteria Anabaena/Nostoc plus Azolla fern (paddy-field nitrogen). They cut urea dependence, build soil life and cost little - green inputs predating the term by centuries (Azolla paddies). Inoculant quality plus correct crop pairing decide field success.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Rhizobium fixes N2; Glomus delivers phosphorus; Azolla feeds paddies.
  • Living inputs build soil; chemicals alone mine it.
Biofertiliser roles
PointDetail
RhizobiumLegume nodule nitrogen
Azospirillum/AzotobacterFree-living nitrogen
Mycorrhiza (Glomus)Phosphorus delivery
Anabaena/AzollaPaddy nitrogen

1

Solution: Fungal hyphae extend phosphorus reach ~100-fold for plant sugars in trade.

Chapter 16 · Topic 10

Biocontrol agents

Microbes in Human WelfareHealth & WelfareStep-by-step Topic
Biocontrol fights pests with living enemies: ladybird beetles and dragonflies eat aphids/mosquitoes; Bt toxin (Bacillus thuringiensis cry proteins, engineered into Bt-cotton) kills caterpillars specifically; baculoviruses (NPV) attack only target insects; Trichoderma fungus suppresses soil pathogens. Benefits: no chemical residues, no resistance treadmill, no friendly-insect killing - the ecological alternative to blanket pesticide sprays.

Related Diagram · Ladybird biocontrol

Fig: Living predators replace pesticide sprays - pests eaten, crops and pollinators unharmed.

Topic in Depth · Biocontrol agents

Biocontrol fights pests with living enemies: ladybird beetles and dragonflies eat aphids and mosquitoes; Bt toxin (Bacillus thuringiensis cry proteins, engineered into Bt-cotton) kills caterpillars specifically; baculoviruses (NPV) attack only target insects; Trichoderma fungus suppresses soil pathogens. Benefits stack - no chemical residues, no resistance treadmill, no friendly-insect killing - the ecological alternative to blanket sprays, though slower acting and needing pest monitoring.
NEET Snapshot:
  • Predators, Bt, viruses, Trichoderma - living pest control.
  • Specific and residue-free, but slower than sprays.
NEET Practice MCQ
1

Bt toxin used in Bt-cotton comes from

Solution: Bacterial cry proteins kill caterpillar pests specifically and safely.
